```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We derive quantitative distortion bounds for countably branching diamond graphs from midpoint estimates and direct tree energies. In the dual of a finite-height segment forest, every distortion-<i>D</i> embedding of the depth-<i>k</i> diamond satisfies $`D^2\\ge1+k/4`$. The same bound holds in the infinite-height coordinate predual. We also obtain power-type distortion bounds for path-cost and recursive tree norms.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We derive quantitative distortion bounds for countably branching diamond graphs from midpoint estimates and direct tree energies. In the dual of a finite-height segment forest, every distortion-<i>D</i> embedding of the depth-<i>k</i> diamond satisfies $`D^2\ge1+k/4`$. The same bound holds in the infinite-height coordinate predual. We also obtain power-type distortion bounds for path-cost and recursive tree norms.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-met-joint-distortion)): **Theorem 1**. *For every integer $k\ge0$, every distortion-$D$ embedding of $\mathcal D_k$ into either $X$ or $B_\infty$ satisfies $$D^2\ge1+k/4.$$*
  - Explanation: The pooled quadratic tail budget forces squared distortion to grow with diamond depth in the specified forest dual and infinite-height coordinate span.
- **C2** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-finite-pair)): Let $X$ be a Banach space, $R>0$, and let $(z_i)_{i\in\mathbb N}$ satisfy $\\|x\pm z_i\\|\le R$ with $\\|z_i-z_j\\|\ge\varepsilon>0$ for $i\ne j$. Every half-difference $w=(z_i-z_j)/2$ with $i\ne j$ satisfies $\\|w\\|\ge\varepsilon/2$ and $\\|x\pm w\\|\le R$. Fix $\eta>0$. The following two selections are available.* 1. *Suppose $p$ has finite support, $\\|p-x\\|<\eta$, and $P$ is a bounded finite-rank coordinate projection satisfying $Pp=p$. There are distinct $i,j$ for which, with $w=(z_i-z_j)/2$ and $y=w-Pw$, $$\label{met:pair-errors} \\|Pw\\|<\eta,\quad \\|y\\|\ge\varepsilon/2-\eta,\quad \\|p\\|\ge\\|x\\|-\eta,\quad \\|p\pm y\\|\le R+2\eta.$$* 2. *If $F$ is a closed finite-codimensional subspace, there are distinct $i,j$ whose half-difference satisfies $\operatorname{dist}(w,F)<\eta$. This selection requires no coordinate structure on $X$.
  - Explanation: Finite-dimensional compactness selects a separated half-difference with a small coordinate head or small distance to a finite-codimensional subspace.
- **C3** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-joint-lens-lemma)): In the finite-height segment forest dual, every infinite $\varepsilon$-separated symmetric $R$-lens satisfies $$\label{met:joint-lens} \\|x\\|^2+\varepsilon^2/16\le R^2.$$ The same lens conclusion holds for the infinite-height closed coordinate span with its own segment-dual norm and finite-head approximation theorem.
  - Explanation: The arbitrary-displacement tail estimate yields a squared center deficit for an infinite separated symmetric lens in each specified target.
- **C4** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-dir-atomic-atoms)): The spaces $J$ and $X$ are reflexive, and finite coordinate arrays are dense in both. If a set $B\subset T$ meets each segment in either a segment or the empty set, its coordinate projection $P_B$ is contractive on both spaces. For an antichain $V$, the coordinate space supported on $V$ has its $\ell_2(V)$ norm in both spaces. In particular, $$\label{dir:atomic:atom-bound} \left\\|\sum_{I\in\mathcal I}\lambda_I s_I^*\right\\|_X \le\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal I}\lambda_I^2\right)^{1/2}$$ for every disjoint segment family. Call a vector on the left with $\sum_I\lambda_I^2\le1$ an atom. The unit ball of $X$ is the norm-closed convex hull of the atoms. If $S$ is finite and ancestral, the unit ball of $P_SX$ is the convex hull of atoms whose segments lie in $S$; every point has a finite convex representation.
  - Explanation: The finite-height forest has reflexive primal and dual spaces, contractive coordinate restrictions, and a disjoint-segment atomic description of its dual unit ball.
- **C5** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-dir-root-correction)): Suppose $v,u\in c_{00}(T)$, $v=P_Av$, and $u=Q_Au$, where $A$ is finite and ancestral. There is $c=Q_Ac\in c_{00}(T)$ such that $$\label{dir:correction-cost} \rho(c)\le\rho(u),\qquad \rho(v+c+r u)^2\le\rho(v)^2+16\rho(u)^2 \quad(r\in\{-1,1\}).$$
  - Explanation: A correction on the first exterior roots controls the squared cost of both signed primal-test updates.
- **C6** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-dir-deterministic-descent)): Every embedding of $\mathcal D_k$ into $X$ with distortion at most $D$ satisfies $k\le256D^2$.
  - Explanation: Following actual child edges increases the primal evaluation while paying a bounded squared test cost, forcing a depth obstruction.
- **C7** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-dir-bin-descent)): Every noncontracting $D$-Lipschitz embedding of $\mathcal D_k$ into $X$ satisfies $D>\sqrt{k}/144$ for $k\ge1$.
  - Explanation: Random descent and dyadic prefix balancing bound the final primal test and force distortion growth.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-dir-atomic-obstruction)): For the real space $X$ defined by <a href="#dir:atomic:forest-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dir:atomic:forest-norm">[dir:atomic:forest-norm]</a>, no finite $D$ has the following property: for every $k\ge1$ there are a map $f_k:\mathcal D_k\to X$ and a scalar $a_k>0$ such that $$a_k d_k(u,v)\le\\|f_k(u)-f_k(v)\\|_X\le D a_k d_k(u,v) \qquad(u,v\in\mathcal D_k).$$
  - Explanation: The segment forest dual cannot accommodate the entire diamond family at a common finite distortion.
- **C9** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-dir-atomic-preparation)): Suppose a depth-$k$ embedding satisfies <a href="#dir:atomic:normalization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dir:atomic:normalization">[dir:atomic:normalization]</a>. For every $0<\eta<1/(4D)$ one can select a fair binary slope martingale $(M_i)_{i=0}^k$, nested finite ancestral sets $S_0\subseteq\cdots\subseteq S_k$, and a finitely valued random atom $Z$ supported in $S_k$, such that, with $E_i=\mathbb E[\,\cdot\mid\mathcal F_i]$ and $U_i=E_iZ=P_{S_k}M_i$, $$\label{dir:atomic:preparation-bounds} \\|Q_{S_{i-1}}U_{i-1}\\|<\eta,\qquad \\|P_{S_i\setminus S_{i-1}}U_i\\|\ge1/D-4\eta \quad(1\le i\le k).$$ The unprojected slopes also satisfy $\\|Q_{S_i}M_i\\|<\eta$ for $0\le i\le k$. Every $M_i$ and $U_i$ has norm at most one. The additional terminal sampling used to produce $Z$ is excluded from each $\mathcal F_i$, including $\mathcal F_k$.
  - Explanation: Coordinate selections produce a finite fair slope martingale with large fresh layers and an exact terminal atomic law; terminal atom sampling remains outside the binary filtration.
- **C10** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-tail-to-lens)): Let $X$ have a specified coordinate system with dense finite-support vectors. Fix a family $\mathcal P$ of bounded finite-rank coordinate projections such that every finite support is contained in the range of some $P\in\mathcal P$. Consider separately the following two hypotheses, each imposed for every $P\in\mathcal P$, all pure head and tail vectors $p=Pp$, $Py=0$, and every $R>0$: $$\begin{aligned} \\|y\\|^2&\le C^2 R(R-\\|p\\|) &&\text{if }\\|p\pm y\\|\le R, \label{met:radial-input}\\ \\|p\\|^2+a\\|y\\|^2&\le R^2 &&\text{if }\\|p\pm y\\|\le R. \label{met:square-input} \end{aligned}$$ Here $C>0$, respectively $a>0$, is uniform over $\mathcal P$, the vectors, and $R$. No uniform bound on $\\|P\\|$ or $\\|I-P\\|$ is assumed. For $R>0$ and $\varepsilon>0$, an infinite $\varepsilon$-separated family in the symmetric $R$-lens at $x$ then satisfies, respectively, $$\begin{aligned} \varepsilon^2/4&\le C^2R(R-\\|x\\|),\label{met:radial-lens}\\ \\|x\\|^2+a\varepsilon^2/4&\le R^2.\label{met:square-lens} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Pure head–tail estimates transfer to radial or squared deficits for arbitrary lens centers using finite-coordinate approximation.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-met-squared-transfer)): If every infinite $\varepsilon$-separated symmetric $R$-lens satisfies $\\|x\\|^2\le R^2-b\varepsilon^2$ for some $b>0$, then every embedding <a href="#met:all-pairs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="met:all-pairs">[met:all-pairs]</a> satisfies $D^2\ge1+4bk$. If instead <a href="#met:radial-lens" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="met:radial-lens">[met:radial-lens]</a> holds, then $$\label{met:additive-bound} k\le C^2D(D-1).$$
  - Explanation: Squared deficits telescope across replacement stages; radial deficits instead give an additive depth bound.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-met-power-transfer)): Assume the density and projection-family hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#met:tail-to-lens" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="met:tail-to-lens">10</a>. Let $q\ge2$ and $c>0$. Suppose that for every admissible projection, every pure head–tail pair $p,y$, and every $R>0$, $$\\|p\pm y\\|\le R \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \\|p\\|^2+cR^{2-q}\\|y\\|^q\le R^2.$$ Then every distortion-$D$ embedding of $\mathcal D_k$ satisfies $$ck\le D^{q-2}(D^2-1).$$
  - Explanation: Retaining the child radius in a homogeneous power loss yields the stated power-dependent distortion obstruction.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-parametric)): Assume the density and projection-family hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#met:tail-to-lens" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="met:tail-to-lens">10</a>. Fix constants $a,b>0$. Suppose that for every admissible projection and every pure head–tail pair $p,y$, the following estimate holds for all $\lambda>0$: $$\max_\pm\\|p\pm y\\|\ge \frac{\\|p\\|+a\lambda\\|y\\|}{\sqrt{1+b\lambda^2}}.$$ Put $Q=\sqrt{1+b\lambda^2}$. For noncontracting diamond slopes bounded above by $D$, the stage suprema satisfy $$R_j\le Q R_{j+1}-a\lambda \le R_{j+1}-\bigl(a\lambda-(Q-1)D\bigr).$$ In particular, whenever $\Delta=a\lambda-(Q-1)D>0$, one has $1+k\Delta\le D$.
  - Explanation: A parameterized primal gain survives finite-head pairing and produces an additive loss whenever the chosen increment is positive.
- **C14** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-backward)): Fix $0<\alpha\le1$. Suppose some $0\le\rho<1$ has the following property: whenever $\\|x\pm z_i\\|\le1$ and $\\|z_i-z_j\\|\ge2\alpha$ for $i\ne j$, one has $\\|x\\|\le\rho$. An embedding with lower factor $\alpha$ and upper factor one must satisfy $$\label{met:backward-bound} \alpha\le\rho^k,$$ where the empty product $\rho^0$ is one.
  - Explanation: A uniform separated-lens contraction propagates backward through the historical edge slopes.
- **C15** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-bridge-enlarged-backward)): If, for every unit $u$, the enlarged lens $$M(u,\delta)=\{z:\max_\pm\\|u\pm z\\|\le1+\delta\}$$ contains no infinite $2/D$-separated family, then every such embedding satisfies $$\label{met:bridge:enlarged-depth} 1\le \frac{D}{(1+\delta)^k}.$$
  - Explanation: Excluding separated enlarged lenses bounds depth through backward contraction or selection of an actual stable parent.
- **C16** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-packing)): Let $P$ be bounded and finite rank, let $A\subset X$ be bounded, and let $b\ge0$ and $\varepsilon>0$. If $\\|(I-P)v\\|\le b$ for all $v\in A$, then $A$ contains no infinite $\varepsilon$-separated family when $2b<\varepsilon$. Consequently the following two inputs have different graph conclusions.* 1. *Let $0<\delta<1$. For each $\\|x\\|>1-\delta$, suppose one such $P$ bounds by $b(\delta)$ the tails of every $v$ with $\\|x\pm v\\|\le1$. If $2b(\delta)<2/D$, backward contraction gives $D^{-1}\le(1-\delta)^k$.* 2. *Let $\delta>0$. For every unit $x$, suppose one such $P$ bounds by $b(\delta)$ the tails of every $v$ with $\\|x\pm v\\|\le1+\delta$. If $2b(\delta)<2/D$, actual-child selection gives $D\ge(1+\delta)^k$.* *The same conclusions hold when the bound is on the first endpoint of every pair with $(u+v)/2=x$: take $u,v\in B_X$ in case (1), and $u,v\in(1+\delta)B_X$ in case (2).
  - Explanation: A single bounded finite-rank head with uniformly small tails excludes infinite separated families; the radius convention determines the graph consequence.
- **C17** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-bridge-sequential-lens)): Assume the density and projection-family hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#met:tail-to-lens" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="met:tail-to-lens">10</a>. Suppose that whenever admissible heads $P_n\in\mathcal P$ and vectors $p_n,y_n$ satisfy $$P_np_n=p_n,\qquad P_ny_n=0,\qquad \\|p_n\pm y_n\\|\le1,\qquad \\|p_n\\|\longrightarrow1,$$ one has $\\|y_n\\|\longrightarrow0$. The heads are allowed to vary with $n$. Then, for each $\varepsilon>0$, there is $0<\rho<1$ such that every infinite $\varepsilon$-separated symmetric unit lens has center norm at most $\rho$.
  - Explanation: Uniform sequential vanishing for varying admissible heads implies a uniform separated-lens gap without an explicit modulus.
- **C18** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-quotient-transfer)): Suppose that for some $t>0$ and $g>0$, every unit vector $u\in X$ admits a closed finite-codimensional subspace $F_u$ such that $$y\in F_u,\quad\\|y\\|\ge t \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \max\{\\|u+y\\|,\\|u-y\\|\}\ge1+g.$$ If $D\ge1$ and $t\le1/(2D)$, every noncontracting embedding of $\mathcal D_k$ with upper factor $D$ satisfies $D\ge(1+g/2)^k$.
  - Explanation: A finite-codimensional midpoint gain selects actual children with a reserved approximation margin and yields multiplicative distortion growth.
- **C19** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-full-gain)): Assume the finite-codimensional hypothesis of Proposition <a href="#met:quotient-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="met:quotient-transfer">18</a>. Consider a noncontracting embedding with upper factor $D\ge1$. If $t\le1/D$ and all child slopes of one parent have norm at most $L$, then $\\|x\\|\le L/(1+g)$. Consequently every depth-$k$ embedding with upper factor $D$ satisfies $1\le D/(1+g)^k$.
  - Explanation: Norm-preserving quotient approximation retains the full asymptotic gain in a backward comparison with the child bound.
- **C20** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-bridge-local-amuc)): Let $R>0$ and put $t=R/4$. Suppose $\gamma>0$ is such that every unit $u$ admits a closed finite-codimensional subspace $F_u$ with $$\label{met:bridge:local-amuc-input} \max_\pm\\|u\pm t y\\|\ge1+\gamma \qquad(y\in F_u,\ \\|y\\|=1).$$ Then $\beta=\gamma/2$ excludes infinite $R$-separated families in $M(u,\beta)$, uniformly over unit $u$.
  - Explanation: A local finite-codimensional estimate excludes separated families in enlarged unit-center lenses.
- **C21** (theorem, [source](#stmt-met-bridge-equivalent-amuc)): If a Banach space admits an equivalent asymptotically midpoint uniformly convex norm, then the family $(\mathcal D_k)_{k\ge0}$ does not embed into it with uniformly bounded distortion. Write $\operatorname{cof}(X)$ for the closed finite-codimensional linear subspaces of $X$. In infinite dimension, midpoint convexity may be expressed by positivity of the maximum modulus $$\label{met:bridge:max-modulus} \delta_N^{\max}(t)= \inf_{N(u)=1}\ \sup_{F\in\operatorname{cof}(X)}\ \inf_{\substack{y\in F\\N(y)=1}} \bigl(\max_\pm N(u\pm t y)-1\bigr)>0 \qquad(0<t<1).$$
  - Explanation: An equivalent asymptotically midpoint uniformly convex norm obstructs uniformly bounded diamond distortion; the maximum modulus records the convention used.
- **C22** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-f-sixth)): For $X_{\mathrm v}$ one has $$\label{met:f:sixth-input} S-2\\|x\\|_{\mathrm v}^2 \ge c_6S^{-2}\\|y\\|_{\mathrm v}^6, \qquad S=\\|x+y\\|_{\mathrm v}^2+\\|x-y\\|_{\mathrm v}^2, \qquad c_6=2\exp(-(\mathrm e-1))4^{-6},$$ whenever $x$ is supported on a finite head and $y$ vanishes there. The right side is interpreted as zero when $S=0$. Every distortion-$D$ embedding of $\mathcal D_k$ into this norm satisfies $$\label{met:f:sixth-power-depth} \frac{c_6 k}{8}\le D^6-D^4.$$ For $0<\varepsilon\le1$, put $$\label{met:f:sixth-q} q_6(\varepsilon)= \left(1-\frac{c_6\varepsilon^6}{512}\right)^{1/2}.$$ Every infinite family satisfying $$\\|x+u_j\\|_{\mathrm v},\ \\|x-u_j\\|_{\mathrm v}\le1, \qquad \\|u_j-u_l\\|_{\mathrm v}\ge\varepsilon\quad(j\ne l)$$ has $\\|x\\|_{\mathrm v}\le q_6(\varepsilon)$. Keeping a fixed unit-lens contraction gives the additional form $$\label{met:f:sixth-depth} D^{-1}\le q_6(D^{-1})^k.$$
  - Explanation: The finite-head sixth-power estimate yields an additive power budget and a separate fixed-separation contraction for the recursive upper-array sum.
- **C23** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-f-cubic-weak)): The dual $X_{\mathrm v}^*$ is separable, and under the same finite-head support conditions as above one has $$\label{met:f:cubic-input} \\|x\pm y\\|_{\mathrm v}\le R \quad\Longrightarrow\quad R^2\ge\\|x\\|_{\mathrm v}^2+ \frac{c_3}{2\sqrt2\,R}\\|y\\|_{\mathrm v}^3, \qquad R>0,\quad c_3=(6\mathrm e)^{-3}.$$ Consequently every distortion-$D$ embedding of $\mathcal D_k$ satisfies $$\label{met:f:cubic-power-depth} \frac{c_3 k}{2\sqrt2}\le D^3-D.$$ For $0<\varepsilon\le1$, define $$\label{met:f:cubic-theta} \theta(\varepsilon)= \left(1-\frac{c_3\varepsilon^3}{2\sqrt2}\right)^{1/2}.$$ If $(h_j)$ is weakly null, $\\|h_j\\|_{\mathrm v}\ge\varepsilon$, and $\\|x\pm h_j\\|_{\mathrm v}\le1$, then $\\|x\\|_{\mathrm v}\le\theta(\varepsilon)$. Every distortion-$D$ embedding of the depth-$k$ countably branching diamond into $X_{\mathrm v}$ satisfies $$\label{met:f:cubic-depth} 1\le D\theta(D^{-1})^k.$$
  - Explanation: The finite-head cubic inequality yields the additive cubic budget. Dual separability supplies weakly null paired differences for the separate geometric contraction.
- **C24** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-f-sum)): For every $0<\varepsilon\le2$ there is $\eta(\varepsilon)\in(0,1)$ such that $$N_J(x\pm z_j)\le1, \qquad N_J(z_j-z_l)\ge\varepsilon\quad(j\ne l) \quad\Longrightarrow\quad N_J(x)\le1-\eta(\varepsilon).$$ For $\varepsilon>2$ the hypotheses are impossible, so any $\eta(\varepsilon)\in(0,1)$ is admissible. An embedding of the depth-$k$ countably branching diamond into $X_J$ with distortion at most $D$ therefore satisfies $$\label{met:f:sum-depth} D^{-1}\le\bigl(1-\eta(2/D)\bigr)^k.$$
  - Explanation: Positivity of propagation for the free-root joined-tree sum gives a qualitative separated-lens gap and its backward depth obstruction.
- **C25** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-f-zero)): If the depth-$k$ countably branching diamond admits an embedding into $X_0$ with distortion at most $D\ge1$, then $$\label{met:f:zero-depth} k\le4608D^3(1-D^{-1}).$$
  - Explanation: The fixed-zero-root joined-tree norm has an explicit cubic depth obstruction obtained by retaining finite approximation errors.
- **C26** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-atomic-distortion)): For the segment forest space $X$, every map satisfying <a href="#met:all-pairs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="met:all-pairs">[met:all-pairs]</a> has $D^2\ge1+k/16$.
  - Explanation: The radial atomic estimate supplies a separate squared-distortion lower bound for the segment forest dual.
- **C27** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-bridge-fixed-bound)): If a target satisfies <a href="#met:bridge:fixed-bound-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="met:bridge:fixed-bound-input">[met:bridge:fixed-bound-input]</a>, every noncontracting embedding of $\mathcal D_k$ with upper factor $D$ obeys $$1+kc_D\le D.$$
  - Explanation: A uniform additive gain for separated bounded deviations forces depth loss by following actual children.
- **C28** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-met-bridge-normer-first)): For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $0<\rho<1$ such that an infinite $\varepsilon$-separated symmetric unit lens in the segment forest space has center norm at most $\rho$.
  - Explanation: Choosing a primal near-normer before the head and the pair yields a qualitative uniform lens gap.

The following two selections are available.*\n\n\n1.  *Suppose $p$ has finite support, $\\|p-x\\|<\\eta$, and $P$ is a bounded finite-rank coordinate projection satisfying $Pp=p$. There are distinct $i,j$ for which, with $w=(z_i-z_j)/2$ and $y=w-Pw$, $$\\label{met:pair-errors}\n     \\|Pw\\|<\\eta,\\quad\n     \\|y\\|\\ge\\varepsilon/2-\\eta,\\quad\n     \\|p\\|\\ge\\|x\\|-\\eta,\\quad\n    \\|p\\pm y\\|\\le R+2\\eta.$$*\n\n2.  *If $F$ is a closed finite-codimensional subspace, there are distinct $i,j$ whose half-difference satisfies $\\operatorname{dist}(w,F)<\\eta$. This selection requires no coordinate structure on $X$.",
```json
          "source_id": "PM-331.3"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-met-finite-pair"
    },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-dir-atomic-atoms",
        "exact_quote": "The spaces $J$ and $X$ are reflexive, and finite coordinate arrays are dense in both. If a set $B\\subset T$ meets each segment in either a segment or the empty set, its coordinate projection $P_B$ is contractive on both spaces. For an antichain $V$, the coordinate space supported on $V$ has its $\\ell_2(V)$ norm in both spaces. In particular, $$\\label{dir:atomic:atom-bound}\n \\left\\|\\sum_{I\\in\\mathcal I}\\lambda_I s_I^*\\right\\|_X\n \\le\\left(\\sum_{I\\in\\mathcal I}\\lambda_I^2\\right)^{1/2}$$ for every disjoint segment family. Call a vector on the left with $\\sum_I\\lambda_I^2\\le1$ an atom. The unit ball of $X$ is the norm-closed convex hull of the atoms. If $S$ is finite and ancestral, the unit ball of $P_SX$ is the convex hull of atoms whose segments lie in $S$; every point has a finite convex representation.",
        "source_id": "PM-331.3"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Suppose a depth-$k$ embedding satisfies <a href=\"#dir:atomic:normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dir:atomic:normalization\">[dir:atomic:normalization]</a>.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-met-tail-to-lens",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ have a specified coordinate system with dense finite-support vectors. Fix a family $\\mathcal P$ of bounded finite-rank coordinate projections such that every finite support is contained in the range of some $P\\in\\mathcal P$. Consider separately the following two hypotheses, each imposed for every $P\\in\\mathcal P$, all pure head and tail vectors $p=Pp$, $Py=0$, and every $R>0$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|y\\|^2&\\le C^2 R(R-\\|p\\|)\n &&\\text{if }\\|p\\pm y\\|\\le R, \\label{met:radial-input}\\\\\n \\|p\\|^2+a\\|y\\|^2&\\le R^2\n &&\\text{if }\\|p\\pm y\\|\\le R. \\label{met:square-input}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $C>0$, respectively $a>0$, is uniform over $\\mathcal P$, the vectors, and $R$. No uniform bound on $\\|P\\|$ or $\\|I-P\\|$ is assumed. For $R>0$ and $\\varepsilon>0$, an infinite $\\varepsilon$-separated family in the symmetric $R$-lens at $x$ then satisfies, respectively, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\varepsilon^2/4&\\le C^2R(R-\\|x\\|),\\label{met:radial-lens}\\\\\n \\|x\\|^2+a\\varepsilon^2/4&\\le R^2.\\label{met:square-lens}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-331.3"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-met-power-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the density and projection-family hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#met:tail-to-lens\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"met:tail-to-lens\">10</a>.         "anchor": "stmt-met-parametric",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the density and projection-family hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#met:tail-to-lens\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"met:tail-to-lens\">10</a>. Fix constants $a,b>0$. Suppose that for every admissible projection and every pure head–tail pair $p,y$, the following estimate holds for all $\\lambda>0$: $$\\max_\\pm\\|p\\pm y\\|\\ge\n \\frac{\\|p\\|+a\\lambda\\|y\\|}{\\sqrt{1+b\\lambda^2}}.$$ Put $Q=\\sqrt{1+b\\lambda^2}$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-met-bridge-enlarged-backward",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D\\ge1$ and $\\delta>0$. If $(1+\\delta)^k>D$, every noncontracting embedding with upper factor $D$ has a parent edge all of whose child slopes have norm at most $(1+\\delta)$ times its own slope norm. If, for every unit $u$, the enlarged lens $$M(u,\\delta)=\\{z:\\max_\\pm\\|u\\pm z\\|\\le1+\\delta\\}$$ contains no infinite $2/D$-separated family, then every such embedding satisfies $$\\label{met:bridge:enlarged-depth}\n 1\\le \\frac{D}{(1+\\delta)^k}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-331.3"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-met-packing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be bounded and finite rank, let $A\\subset X$ be bounded, and let $b\\ge0$ and $\\varepsilon>0$. If $\\|(I-P)v\\|\\le b$ for all $v\\in A$, then $A$ contains no infinite $\\varepsilon$-separated family when $2b<\\varepsilon$. Consequently the following two inputs have different graph conclusions.*\n\n1.  *Let $0<\\delta<1$. For each $\\|x\\|>1-\\delta$, suppose one such $P$ bounds by $b(\\delta)$ the tails of every $v$ with $\\|x\\pm v\\|\\le1$. If $2b(\\delta)<2/D$, backward contraction gives $D^{-1}\\le(1-\\delta)^k$.*\n\n2.  *Let $\\delta>0$. For every unit $x$, suppose one such $P$ bounds by $b(\\delta)$ the tails of every $v$ with $\\|x\\pm v\\|\\le1+\\delta$. If $2b(\\delta)<2/D$, actual-child selection gives $D\\ge(1+\\delta)^k$.*\n\n*The same conclusions hold when the bound is on the first endpoint of every pair with $(u+v)/2=x$: take $u,v\\in B_X$ in case (1), and $u,v\\in(1+\\delta)B_X$ in case (2).",
        "source_id": "PM-331.3"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-met-bridge-sequential-lens",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the density and projection-family hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#met:tail-to-lens\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"met:tail-to-lens\">10</a>. Suppose that whenever admissible heads $P_n\\in\\mathcal P$ and vectors $p_n,y_n$ satisfy $$P_np_n=p_n,\\qquad P_ny_n=0,\\qquad\n \\|p_n\\pm y_n\\|\\le1,\\qquad \\|p_n\\|\\longrightarrow1,$$ one has $\\|y_n\\|\\longrightarrow0$. The heads are allowed to vary with $n$. Then, for each $\\varepsilon>0$, there is $0<\\rho<1$ such that every infinite $\\varepsilon$-separated symmetric unit lens has center norm at most $\\rho$.",
        "source_id": "PM-331.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-met-quotient-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that for some $t>0$ and $g>0$, every unit vector $u\\in X$ admits a closed finite-codimensional subspace $F_u$ such that $$y\\in F_u,\\quad\\|y\\|\\ge t\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\max\\{\\|u+y\\|,\\|u-y\\|\\}\\ge1+g.$$ If $D\\ge1$ and $t\\le1/(2D)$, every noncontracting embedding of $\\mathcal D_k$ with upper factor $D$ satisfies $D\\ge(1+g/2)^k$.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-met-bridge-equivalent-amuc",
        "exact_quote": "If a Banach space admits an equivalent asymptotically midpoint uniformly convex norm, then the family $(\\mathcal D_k)_{k\\ge0}$ does not embed into it with uniformly bounded distortion. Write $\\operatorname{cof}(X)$ for the closed finite-codimensional linear subspaces of $X$. In infinite dimension, midpoint convexity may be expressed by positivity of the maximum modulus $$\\label{met:bridge:max-modulus}\n \\delta_N^{\\max}(t)=\n \\inf_{N(u)=1}\\ \n \\sup_{F\\in\\operatorname{cof}(X)}\\ \n \\inf_{\\substack{y\\in F\\\\N(y)=1}}\n \\bigl(\\max_\\pm N(u\\pm t y)-1\\bigr)>0\n \\qquad(0<t<1).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-331.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-met-bridge-equivalent-amuc",
          "exact_quote": "If a Banach space admits an equivalent asymptotically midpoint uniformly convex norm, then the family $(\\mathcal D_k)_{k\\ge0}$ does not embed into it with uniformly bounded distortion. Write $\\operatorname{cof}(X)$ for the closed finite-codimensional linear subspaces of $X$. In infinite dimension, midpoint convexity may be expressed by positivity of the maximum modulus $$\\label{met:bridge:max-modulus}\n \\delta_N^{\\max}(t)=\n \\inf_{N(u)=1}\\ \n \\sup_{F\\in\\operatorname{cof}(X)}\\ \n \\inf_{\\substack{y\\in F\\\\N(y)=1}}\n \\bigl(\\max_\\pm N(u\\pm t y)-1\\bigr)>0\n \\qquad(0<t<1).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-331.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "If a Banach space admits an equivalent asymptotically midpoint uniformly convex norm, then the family $(\\mathcal D_k)_{k\\ge0}$ does not embed into it with uniformly bounded distortion.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-met-f-sixth",
        "exact_quote": "For $X_{\\mathrm v}$ one has $$\\label{met:f:sixth-input}\n S-2\\|x\\|_{\\mathrm v}^2\n \\ge c_6S^{-2}\\|y\\|_{\\mathrm v}^6,\n \\qquad\n S=\\|x+y\\|_{\\mathrm v}^2+\\|x-y\\|_{\\mathrm v}^2,\n \\qquad c_6=2\\exp(-(\\mathrm e-1))4^{-6},$$ whenever $x$ is supported on a finite head and $y$ vanishes there. The right side is interpreted as zero when $S=0$. Every distortion-$D$ embedding of $\\mathcal D_k$ into this norm satisfies $$\\label{met:f:sixth-power-depth}\n \\frac{c_6 k}{8}\\le D^6-D^4.$$ For $0<\\varepsilon\\le1$, put $$\\label{met:f:sixth-q}\n q_6(\\varepsilon)=\n \\left(1-\\frac{c_6\\varepsilon^6}{512}\\right)^{1/2}.$$ Every infinite family satisfying $$\\|x+u_j\\|_{\\mathrm v},\\ \\|x-u_j\\|_{\\mathrm v}\\le1,\n \\qquad \\|u_j-u_l\\|_{\\mathrm v}\\ge\\varepsilon\\quad(j\\ne l)$$ has $\\|x\\|_{\\mathrm v}\\le q_6(\\varepsilon)$. Keeping a fixed unit-lens contraction gives the additional form $$\\label{met:f:sixth-depth}\n D^{-1}\\le q_6(D^{-1})^k.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-331.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-met-f-sixth",
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Normalized diamond graph** ([source](#met:graph-definition)): Let $\mathcal D_0$ consist of two vertices joined by an edge of length one. To obtain $\mathcal D_{j+1}$, replace every edge $[a,b]$ of $\mathcal D_j$ by paths $[a,m_i,b]$, indexed by $i\in\mathbb N$, whose middle vertices are distinct and new. Both new edges have half the length of their parent.
- **Shortest-path metric** ([source](#met:graph-definition)): Let $d_k$ be the shortest-path metric on the vertices of $\mathcal D_k$.
- **Symmetric lens** ([source](#met:section)): For $x\in X$ and $R\ge0$, write $\mathcal L_R(x)=\{z\in X:\\|x+z\\|\le R,\ \\|x-z\\|\le R\}$ for the symmetric $R$-lens at $x$.
- **Embedding distortion** ([source](#met:introduction)): A map $f$ has distortion at most $D$ if some $s>0$ satisfies $$s d_k(u,v)\le\\|f(u)-f(v)\\|\le Ds d_k(u,v) \qquad(u,v\in\mathcal D_k).$$
- **Segment forest** ([source](#dir:atomic:forest-norm)): Let $$T=\coprod_{n\ge1} T_n,\qquad T_n=\mathbb N^{\le n},$$ where each component has its own root and is ordered by extension.
- **Segment** ([source](#dir:atomic:forest-norm)): A segment is a set $[a,b]=\{t:a\le t\le b\}$ with $a\le b$ in one component.
- **Ancestral set** ([source](#dir:atomic:forest-norm)): A set is ancestral if it contains every ancestor of each of its vertices.
- **Disjoint-segment norm** ([source](#dir:atomic:forest-norm)): On real finitely supported arrays define $$\label{dir:atomic:forest-norm} \rho(y)= \sup_{\mathcal I} \left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal I}\left\|\sum_{t\in I}y(t)\right\|^2\right)^{1/2},$$ where the supremum runs over finite disjoint families of segments.
- **Forest primal and dual** ([source](#dir:atomic:forest-norm)): Let $J$ be its completion and $X=J^*$.
- **Infinite-height coordinate span** ([source](#met:introduction)): Define $$B_\infty=\overline{\operatorname{span}}\{e_t^*:t\in T_\infty\} \subset J_\infty^*,$$ where $e_t^*$ is coordinate evaluation and the closure uses the dual norm.
- **Ancestral head and tail projections** ([source](#met:introduction)): A finite set $H$ is ancestral if it contains every ancestor of each of its vertices; $P_H$ retains its coordinates and $Q_H=I-P_H$.
- **Disjoint-segment atom** ([source](#stmt-dir-atomic-atoms)): Call a vector on the left with $\sum_I\lambda_I^2\le1$ an atom.
- **Parent slope and deviations** ([source](#met:slopes)): Orient the original edge and orient every replacement path consistently. For a parent edge $[a,b]$ of represented length $\ell$, define $$\label{met:slopes} x=\frac{f(b)-f(a)}{\ell},\qquad z_i=\frac{2}{\ell}\left(f(m_i)-\frac{f(a)+f(b)}2\right).$$ Its child slopes are $x+z_i$ and $x-z_i$.
- **Linear path-cost norm** ([source](#dir:layers)): For $T_n=\mathbb N^{\le n}$, including its root, let $b_t=\sum_{s\preceq t}e_s$ and $$L_n(z)=\inf_{z=h+\sum_t\alpha_tb_t} \bigl(\\|h\\|_{\ell_2(T_n)}+\\|\alpha\\|_{\ell_1(T_n)}\bigr), \qquad X_{\rm lin}=\left(\bigoplus_{n\ge1}(\ell_2(T_n),L_n)\right)_2.$$ The infimum ranges over real $h\in\ell_2(T_n)$ and $\alpha\in\ell_1(T_n)$; it need not be attained.
- **Quadratic path-cost norm** ([source](#met:quadratic-input)): On the same trees define $$Q_n(z)=\inf_{z=h+J_n\mu}(\\|h\\|_2^2+\\|\mu\\|_1^2)^{1/2},\qquad X_{\rm quad}=\left(\bigoplus_{n\ge1}(\ell_2(T_n),Q_n)\right)_2.$$
- **Recursive upper-array norm** ([source](#met:f:applications)): Write $$\nu_h(v)=P_\emptyset(v)+Q_\emptyset(v),\qquad X_{\mathrm v}=\Big(\bigoplus_{h\ge1}(\ell_{p_h}(T_h),\nu_h) \Big)_{\ell_2}.$$
- **Free-root joined-tree norm** ([source](#pm-h-44)): For a finitely supported vector with an unrestricted root coordinate, put $$N_J(x)=p_\rho(x)+q_\rho(x),$$ and let $X_J$ be the completion.
- **Fixed-zero-root norm** ([source](#pm-h-45)): On the same joined tree, now require $x_\rho=0$ and define $$N_0(x)=p_\rho(x)=q_\rho(x) =\max\left\{ \Big(\sum_h p_{r_h}(x)^2\Big)^{1/2}, \Big(\sum_h q_{r_h}(x)^2\Big)^{1/2}\right\},$$ where $r_h$ is the root of the $h$th component. Denote its completion by $X_0$.
- **Prescribed segment-start cost norm** ([source](#met:segment-section)): For nonnegative start masses $m_s$ on $T_n$, let $q_s=m_s+(\sum_{c\text{ child of }s}q_c^2)^{1/2}$, computed upward from the leaves. A finite or countable signed segment representation $z=\sum_j a_j\mathbf1_{[s_j,t_j]}$, with absolutely convergent output at every coordinate, has start masses $m_s=\sum_{j:s_j=s}\|a_j\|$ and cost $q_o$ at the root. Take the infimum over all such representations of finite cost and complete; the outer Hilbert sum of the resulting finite-height spaces is denoted $X_{\rm start}$.
- **Incomparable-segment norm** ([source](#met:incomparable-section)): Let $Y_I$ be the completion on the disjoint union of $T_n$ for the supremum of Euclidean sums of segment evaluations, where the admissible segments are pairwise incomparable: every node of one segment is incomparable with every node of another. Put $X_I=Y_I^*$.

| Q21 | Sixth-power coefficient | = | $2\exp(-(\mathrm e-1))4^{-6}$ | Finite-head sixth-power estimate. |
| Q22 | Cubic coefficient | = | $(6\mathrm e)^{-3}$ | Finite-head cubic estimate. |

```json
          "anchor": "stmt-met-f-sixth",
          "exact_quote": "For $X_{\\mathrm v}$ one has $$\\label{met:f:sixth-input}\n S-2\\|x\\|_{\\mathrm v}^2\n \\ge c_6S^{-2}\\|y\\|_{\\mathrm v}^6,\n \\qquad\n S=\\|x+y\\|_{\\mathrm v}^2+\\|x-y\\|_{\\mathrm v}^2,\n \\qquad c_6=2\\exp(-(\\mathrm e-1))4^{-6},$$ whenever $x$ is supported on a finite head and $y$ vanishes there. The right side is interpreted as zero when $S=0$. Every distortion-$D$ embedding of $\\mathcal D_k$ into this norm satisfies $$\\label{met:f:sixth-power-depth}\n \\frac{c_6 k}{8}\\le D^6-D^4.$$ For $0<\\varepsilon\\le1$, put $$\\label{met:f:sixth-q}\n q_6(\\varepsilon)=\n \\left(1-\\frac{c_6\\varepsilon^6}{512}\\right)^{1/2}.$$ Every infinite family satisfying $$\\|x+u_j\\|_{\\mathrm v},\\ \\|x-u_j\\|_{\\mathrm v}\\le1,\n \\qquad \\|u_j-u_l\\|_{\\mathrm v}\\ge\\varepsilon\\quad(j\\ne l)$$ has $\\|x\\|_{\\mathrm v}\\le q_6(\\varepsilon)$. Keeping a fixed unit-lens contraction gives the additional form $$\\label{met:f:sixth-depth}\n D^{-1}\\le q_6(D^{-1})^k.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-331.3"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-met-f-cubic-weak",
          "exact_quote": "The dual $X_{\\mathrm v}^*$ is separable, and under the same finite-head support conditions as above one has $$\\label{met:f:cubic-input}\n \\|x\\pm y\\|_{\\mathrm v}\\le R\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n R^2\\ge\\|x\\|_{\\mathrm v}^2+\n       \\frac{c_3}{2\\sqrt2\\,R}\\|y\\|_{\\mathrm v}^3,\n \\qquad R>0,\\quad c_3=(6\\mathrm e)^{-3}.$$ Consequently every distortion-$D$ embedding of $\\mathcal D_k$ satisfies $$\\label{met:f:cubic-power-depth}\n \\frac{c_3 k}{2\\sqrt2}\\le D^3-D.$$ For $0<\\varepsilon\\le1$, define $$\\label{met:f:cubic-theta}\n \\theta(\\varepsilon)=\n \\left(1-\\frac{c_3\\varepsilon^3}{2\\sqrt2}\\right)^{1/2}.$$ If $(h_j)$ is weakly null, $\\|h_j\\|_{\\mathrm v}\\ge\\varepsilon$, and $\\|x\\pm h_j\\|_{\\mathrm v}\\le1$, then $\\|x\\|_{\\mathrm v}\\le\\theta(\\varepsilon)$. Every distortion-$D$ embedding of the depth-$k$ countably branching diamond into $X_{\\mathrm v}$ satisfies $$\\label{met:f:cubic-depth}\n 1\\le D\\theta(D^{-1})^k.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-331.3"
        }

            "exact_quote": "The dual $X_{\\mathrm v}^*$ is separable, and under the same finite-head support conditions as above one has $$\\label{met:f:cubic-input}\n \\|x\\pm y\\|_{\\mathrm v}\\le R\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n R^2\\ge\\|x\\|_{\\mathrm v}^2+\n       \\frac{c_3}{2\\sqrt2\\,R}\\|y\\|_{\\mathrm v}^3,\n \\qquad R>0,\\quad c_3=(6\\mathrm e)^{-3}.$$ Consequently every distortion-$D$ embedding of $\\mathcal D_k$ satisfies $$\\label{met:f:cubic-power-depth}\n \\frac{c_3 k}{2\\sqrt2}\\le D^3-D.$$ For $0<\\varepsilon\\le1$, define $$\\label{met:f:cubic-theta}\n \\theta(\\varepsilon)=\n \\left(1-\\frac{c_3\\varepsilon^3}{2\\sqrt2}\\right)^{1/2}.$$ If $(h_j)$ is weakly null, $\\|h_j\\|_{\\mathrm v}\\ge\\varepsilon$, and $\\|x\\pm h_j\\|_{\\mathrm v}\\le1$, then $\\|x\\|_{\\mathrm v}\\le\\theta(\\varepsilon)$.             "source_id": "PM-331.3"
          }
```

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R2** [claim](#stmt-met-finite-pair) / [proof](#proof-stmt-met-finite-pair): Finite-dimensional pair selection -> A half-difference with negligible head or quotient error.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-met-joint-lens-lemma) / [proof](#proof-stmt-met-joint-lens-lemma): Finite-head approximation and limiting quadratic budget -> Squared center deficit in a separated symmetric lens.
- **R1** [claim](#stmt-met-joint-distortion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-met-joint-lens-lemma-2): Telescope squared stage suprema -> Depth-dependent squared distortion obstruction.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-dir-root-correction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dir-root-correction): Correct first exterior roots and split crossing head segments -> Uniform squared-norm cost for signed test updates.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-dir-deterministic-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dir-deterministic-descent): Accumulate evaluations along actual child edges -> Direct quadratic depth obstruction.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-dir-bin-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dir-bin-descent): Balance dyadic prefix potentials along fair descent -> Bounded accumulated primal test and distortion growth.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-dir-atomic-preparation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dir-atomic-preparation): Select common heads and sample exact terminal atoms -> Fair finite slope martingale with large fresh layers.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-dir-atomic-obstruction) / [proof](#dir:atomic:endpoint-route): Count segment endpoints and telescope coefficient prediction energy -> Contradiction to uniformly bounded distortion.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-met-tail-to-lens) / [proof](#proof-stmt-met-tail-to-lens): Approximate heads, pair deviations, and let the error vanish -> Radial and squared lens estimates.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-met-squared-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-met-squared-transfer): Sum squared or radial losses -> Additive distortion budgets.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-met-power-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-met-power-transfer): Retain child-radius dependence during telescoping -> Power-dependent depth obstruction.
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-met-backward) / [proof](#proof-stmt-met-backward): Iterate parent contraction backward -> Geometric depth obstruction.
- **R18** [claim](#stmt-met-quotient-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-met-quotient-transfer): Reserve error and follow a growing actual child -> Multiplicative distortion growth.
- **R23** [claim](#stmt-met-f-cubic-weak) / [proof](#proof-stmt-met-f-cubic-weak): Separate power transfer from weakly null paired contraction -> Additive cubic budget and separate backward geometric bound.
- **R25** [claim](#stmt-met-f-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-met-f-zero): Retain finite approximation errors in an additive cubic gain -> Explicit cubic depth obstruction.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **root-correction**: A finitely supported correction outside the ancestral head bounds the squared cost of either signed tail update.
- **all-pairs**: The distortion normalization controls every vertex pair, including distinct replacement midpoints.
- **atomic-energy**: Terminal sampling is excluded from the slope filtration, so the projected slopes remain conditional means of the terminal atom.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **finite-branching**: Apply the separated-midpoint obstruction to a finitely branching graph. -> Finite midpoint collections do not provide the infinite compactness selection used in the proof.
- **edge-only**: Check only distances on graph edges before applying the distortion bound. -> Sibling midpoint separation need not follow from edge bounds alone.
- **norm-substitution**: Transfer the numerical constants between linear and quadratic path costs. -> Changing the norm changes its normalization and the intrinsic estimate.
- **attainment**: Select a child presumed to attain the supremum of child norms. -> The supremum need not be attained.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The fresh-root correction is finite and constructive for finitely supported head and tail vectors. This implementation restricts tails to fresh roots, where subtree norms are absolute coordinate values; it does not decide diamond embedding existence.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of finite rooted-tree cases: parent, head, v, u.
# PRECONDITIONS: At most 4 nodes; ancestral head; integer v supported on head; integer u supported on first exterior roots.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of correction arrays c from the shortest qualifying suffix rule.
# TIME: O(number of cases times node count squared).
# SPACE: O(number of cases times node count).
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integers using python-flint fmpz; no floating point.
# FAILURE: Invalid cases raise ValueError; no approximate answer is returned.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz

def correction(case):
    par, H, v, u = case['parent'], set(case['head']), case['v'], case['u']
    n = len(par)
    if not 1 <= n <= 4 or len(v) != n or len(u) != n:
        raise ValueError('size')
    if any(type(a) is not int for a in par + v + u + list(H)):
        raise ValueError('integer input')
    if par[0] != -1 or any(not 0 <= par[i] < i for i in range(1,n)):
        raise ValueError('tree')
    if any(not 0 <= a < n for a in H) or any(par[a] >= 0 and par[a] not in H for a in H):
        raise ValueError('ancestral head')
    fresh = {i for i in range(n) if i not in H and (par[i] == -1 or par[i] in H)}
    if any(v[i] and i not in H for i in range(n)) or any(u[i] and i not in fresh for i in range(n)):
        raise ValueError('support')
    out = [0] * n
    for d in sorted(fresh):
        a = abs(fmpz(u[d]))
        p, total = par[d], fmpz(0)
        while p in H:
            total += fmpz(v[p])
            if abs(total) > 2 * a:
                out[d] = int(-a if total > 0 else a)
                break
            p = par[p]
    return out
print(json.dumps([correction(c) for c in json.load(sys.stdin)]))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | Finite tree norm calculations can inform a numerical model of the specified spaces; the paper supplies no software implementation or termination contract for infinite coordinate selection. |
| games_simulation | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | Finite diamond replacements and fair slope descents can inspire a graph geometry simulation; truncating branching changes the hypotheses of the obstruction. |
| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | Distortion bounds can inform an analytical screening aid for candidate embeddings into the specified norms; satisfying a necessary bound does not construct an embedding. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Recursive upper arrays and finite selected slope descents provide algorithmic patterns, but compactness-based selection is not an effective search routine. |
| data_structures | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | Ancestral coordinate heads and tree-indexed arrays suggest representations for finite norm experiments; the paper states no storage layout or complexity bound. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no accelerator architecture, device arithmetic model or parallel kernel contract is supplied for these norm inequalities. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the embedding obstructions state geometric restrictions and supply no cryptographic construction or security assumption. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no learning objective, training procedure or data model connects the stated target norms to a learned representation. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The infimum definitions specify norms, but an optimisation method, attainment guarantee and operational model are ABSENT. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the finite martingale arguments have no communication, replication or distributed execution contract. |
| other (Banach-space metric geometry) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The direct application is Banach-space metric geometry: midpoint lenses and tree norm energies obstruct uniformly bounded diamond distortion. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **branching** (hypothesis): The midpoint selections require infinitely many replacement midpoints. A finite branching truncation does not supply the compactness selection used by these proofs. Blocked use: ['Transferring the obstruction to arbitrary finite branching graphs.']
- **all-pairs** (hypothesis): The lower embedding inequality applies to all vertex pairs, including distinct replacement midpoints. Checking only graph edges does not establish this hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Certifying distortion from edge lengths alone.']
- **coordinate-predual** (formal_scope): The infinite-height conclusion concerns the closed coordinate span with its own norm and approximation theorem. It does not identify that space with the full infinite-height dual. Blocked use: ['Applying the infinite-height conclusion to the full dual without further proof.']
- **norm-specific** (hypothesis): Linear and quadratic path costs, joining-root restrictions and child aggregation specify different norms. Their quantitative constants remain attached to the corresponding intrinsic inputs. Blocked use: ['Substituting constants between target norms or between independent proofs.']
- **converse** (counterexample_scope): The stated converse results retain reflexivity or unconditional asymptotic structure hypotheses. A diamond obstruction alone does not establish those converse conclusions. Blocked use: ['Inferring an equivalent convex norm solely from the obstruction.']
- **supremum** (nonconstructive): Stage slope suprema need not be attained. A limiting supremum bound cannot be treated as an actual child achieving that bound. Blocked use: ['Extracting a maximizing child from a supremum inequality without reserved slack.']
- **finite-head** (hypothesis): Packing uses a single finite head controlling every member of the lens. Heads selected separately for each deviation do not meet this quantifier order. Blocked use: ['Applying packing to individually chosen tail approximations.']
- **support-timing** (hypothesis): The predictable-layer estimates require ancestral supports fixed before the next sign and exact martingale averaging. Adaptive windows and common level heads have separate hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Applying a supported predictable estimate to unsupported exact slopes.']
- **qualitative-rate** (nonconstructive): The free-root propagation estimate supplies a positive lens gap without a numerical power law. It supports the qualitative depth obstruction in its stated norm. Blocked use: ['Assigning an explicit polynomial distortion rate to the qualitative propagation argument.']
- **sampling-filtration** (hypothesis): The terminal atom sampling is excluded from the slope filtration, including the terminal binary history. Including that extra randomness changes the conditional-mean identity used in the energy arguments. Blocked use: ['Conditioning slope predictions on the sampled terminal atom.']
- **coordinate-null** (formal_scope): Coordinatewise convergence is used with the stated intrinsic coordinate-null estimate. It does not supply weak convergence by itself. Blocked use: ['Replacing coordinate-null arguments by weakly null arguments without another hypothesis.']
- **renorming** (approximation): Equivalent renorming preserves absence of uniform diamond distortion, while norm-specific numerical constants may change. Blocked use: ['Transporting the displayed constants unchanged through equivalent renorming.']
- **finite-certificate** (nonconstructive): The finite binary martingales are formed after selections from infinitely many midpoint candidates. Exhaustive checks on finite selected data do not establish availability of those selections for every embedding. Blocked use: ['Treating finite computations as certificates for the full Banach-space non-embedding theorem.']
- **cubic-routes** (formal_scope): The additive cubic depth bound follows from the finite-head inequality through the power transfer. Dual separability and weakly null paired differences supply the separate geometric contraction. Blocked use: ['Attributing the additive cubic bound to weakly null extraction.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 331 (`lean/docs/331.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Asymptotic midpoint uniform convexity and unbounded diamond distortion in a reflexive tree space](../../preprints/Asymptotic-midpoint-uniform-convexity-and-unbounded-diamond-distortion-in-a-reflexive-tree-space-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Midpoint lenses in segment spaces](../../preprints/Midpoint-lenses-in-segment-spaces-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Distortion of countably branching diamonds from midpoint and tree energies](../../preprints/Diamond-distortion-from-midpoint-and-tree-energies-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Exact asymptotic moduli in a Daugavet subspace of $L_1$](../../preprints/Exact-asymptotic-moduli-in-a-Daugavet-subspace-of-L1-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Midpoint convexity from bounded tree potentials and path costs](../../preprints/Midpoint-convexity-from-bounded-tree-potentials-and-path-costs-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Independent products in real $L_1$: asymptotic midpoint convexity without AUC renormings](../../preprints/Independent-products-in-real-L1-asymptotic-midpoint-convexity-without-AUC-renormings-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Midpoint convexity from two recursive potentials](../../preprints/Midpoint-convexity-from-two-recursive-potentials-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The paper constructs a Banach space whose midpoint geometry is asymptotically uniformly convex even though no equivalent norm is asymptotically uniformly convex in the usual one-sided sense. The formalization proves that the full dual of the specified segment-norm completion is infinite-dimensional, separable, and reflexive; its averaged midpoint modulus is at least $\sqrt{1+t^2/12}-1$ for every $t>0$, while every equivalent norm fails asymptotic uniform convexity. It also proves that an embedding of a depth-$k$ countably branching diamond has distortion at least $\sqrt{1+k/12}$.
>
> The general-forest and word-forest results use the closed span of the coordinate functionals. Their conclusions retain the finite-ancestor and equivalent-norm hypotheses, including the scale $\alpha/(2\beta)$ when $0<\alpha\le\beta$ and the new norm lies between $\alpha$ and $\beta$ times the original norm; they do not identify that span with the full dual or assert reflexivity for every forest.
>
> The paper controls midpoint lenses in Banach spaces defined by tree-segment norms. The formalization proves the following estimate in both the full dual of the finite-height forest space and the coordinate predual of the infinite-tree space. If $x$ is supported on a finite ancestral set $H$, $R\ge0$, and $\|x+y\|,\|x-y\|\le R$, then the part of $y$ outside $H$ has norm at most $2\sqrt{R^2-\|x\|^2}$.
>
> The formalization also covers selected consequences for averaged midpoint moduli and separated families of vectors, together with reflexivity and renorming obstructions. This scope concerns the segment-space results; the companion results on diamond energies remain separate.
>
> The paper studies embeddings of countably branching diamond graphs into Banach spaces built from tree segments. The formalization proves that every embedding of the depth-$k$ diamond with distortion $D$, at any positive scale, satisfies $1+k/4\le D^2$ in either of two spaces: the full dual of the completed finite-height forest space and the closed coordinate span in the dual of the infinite-tree space. The bound applies to all pairs of vertices and is unconditional.
>
> The formalization also contains selected graph deductions under explicit companion assumptions. Twenty-two of those companion inputs are assumed in these deductions; the diamond distortion bound above does not require them.
>
> The paper exhibits a real $L_1$ subspace with positive averaged midpoint convexity but no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm. For every closed infinite-dimensional subspace whose unit ball is precompact in measure and which has the Daugavet property, the formalization computes the moduli at every unit vector: the averaged midpoint modulus is $\max(t/2,t-1)$ and the usual one-sided asymptotic modulus is $\max(0,t-2)$ for every $t>0$. The same formulas hold after taking the infimum over unit vectors, and every equivalent norm fails asymptotic uniform convexity.
>
> The formalization also constructs such a subspace on a countable product of unit intervals and proves that it has the stated measure-precompactness and Daugavet properties, so the modulus formulas apply to an actual example.
>
> The paper constructs Banach spaces from bounded tree potentials and path costs to separate averaged midpoint convexity from asymptotic uniform convexity. For the specified tree-potential completions, the formalization proves completeness, separability, infinite dimension, and an averaged midpoint modulus of at least $\sqrt{1+t^2/4}-1$ for $0<t<1$. No space linearly isomorphic to one of these completions is asymptotically uniformly convex.
>
> The formalization also covers selected path-cost duality and comparison results, clipping and energy estimates, and further renorming obstructions. These retain their stated support, head, and tail hypotheses; the remaining auxiliary assertions of the paper are outside this scope.
>
> The paper forms a real $L_1$ space from the closed span of products of independent multipliers along tree paths. For a positive, nonconstant multiplier of mean one and finite second moment, the formalization proves that this space is infinite-dimensional and has no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm.
>
> For every $t>0$, the averaged midpoint modulus nevertheless has explicit positive lower bounds. If $G$ is a standard real Gaussian, the exponential-multiplier bound is $\frac{7t}{480}\mathbb E[(|G|-20/t)_+]$, where $u_+=\max(u,0)$. For squared-Gaussian multipliers, the bounds are $\frac18\Pr(|G|\ge4\sqrt2(2+\sqrt2)/t)$ and $\frac14\Pr(|G|\ge8\sqrt6/t)$.
>
> The paper constructs three Banach spaces from recursive tree potentials: a root-sum space, a zero-root space, and a joined space. The formalization proves that all three are infinite-dimensional and have no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm. The root-sum and joined spaces have averaged midpoint modulus at least $t^3/128$ for $0<t<1$, and the root-sum modulus is positive for every $t>0$. The zero-root estimate retains its finite ancestral support and tail hypotheses, and the zero-root and joined spaces are reflexive.
>
> The formalization also covers a separated-midpoint result for the joined space and a variable-exponent model with its recursive norm, duality, projections, renorming obstruction, and sixth- and third-order stability estimates. The separation, sequence, support, and head assumptions in these results are essential parts of their statements.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

universe u v


open scoped BigOperators Classical Topology
open Set

namespace DiamondDistortion

structure SegmentSystem (ι : Type*) where
  IsSegment : Finset ι → Prop
  singleton : ∀ v, IsSegment {v}

namespace SegmentSystem

variable {ι : Type*} (T : SegmentSystem ι)

/-- The represented disjoint families in the definition of the primal segment norm. -/
structure Family where
  n : ℕ
  seg : Fin n → Finset ι
  allowed : ∀ i, T.IsSegment (seg i)
  disjoint : Pairwise (fun i j => Disjoint (seg i) (seg j))

instance : Nonempty T.Family := ⟨{
  n := 0
  seg := Fin.elim0
  allowed := fun i => Fin.elim0 i
  disjoint := fun {i} => Fin.elim0 i }⟩

variable {T}

/-- Segment testing as a Euclidean-valued linear map. -/
def Family.test (F : T.Family) : (ι →₀ ℝ) →ₗ[ℝ] EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin F.n) where
  toFun u := WithLp.toLp 2 (fun i => ∑ v ∈ F.seg i, u v)
  map_add' u v := by
    ext i
    simp [Finset.sum_add_distrib]
  map_smul' c u := by
    ext i
    simp [Finset.mul_sum]

def Family.seminorm (F : T.Family) : Seminorm ℝ (ι →₀ ℝ) :=
  (normSeminorm ℝ (EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin F.n))).comp F.test

variable (T)

def primalSeminorm : Seminorm ℝ (ι →₀ ℝ) := ⨆ F : T.Family, F.seminorm

/-- Singleton families ensure that the segment seminorm separates points. -/
def singletonFamily (v : ι) : T.Family where
  n := 1
  seg _ := {v}
  allowed _ := T.singleton v
  disjoint := by intro i j h; exact False.elim (h (Subsingleton.elim _ _))

def Primal (_T : SegmentSystem ι) := ι →₀ ℝ

instance : AddCommGroup T.Primal := inferInstanceAs (AddCommGroup (ι →₀ ℝ))
instance : Module ℝ T.Primal := inferInstanceAs (Module ℝ (ι →₀ ℝ))

instance : NormedAddCommGroup T.Primal := by
  have Family.test_apply (F : T.Family) (u : ι →₀ ℝ) (i : Fin F.n) :
      F.test u i = ∑ v ∈ F.seg i, u v := rfl
  have Family.seminorm_apply (F : T.Family) (u : ι →₀ ℝ) :
      F.seminorm u = ‖F.test u‖ := rfl
  have Family.test_single_le (F : T.Family) (v : ι) (r : ℝ) :
      ‖F.test (Finsupp.single v r)‖ ≤ |r| := by
    classical
    rw [EuclideanSpace.norm_eq]
    apply (Real.sqrt_le_left (abs_nonneg r)).2
    by_cases h : ∃ i, v ∈ F.seg i
    · obtain ⟨i, hi⟩ := h
      have hne : ∀ j, j ≠ i → v ∉ F.seg j := by
        intro j hji hj
        exact Finset.disjoint_left.mp (F.disjoint hji) hj hi
      rw [Finset.sum_eq_single i]
      · simp [Family.test, Finsupp.single_apply, hi]
      · intro j _ hji
        simp [Family.test, Finsupp.single_apply, hne j hji]
      · simp
    · have hn : ∀ i, v ∉ F.seg i := by simpa using h
      simp [Family.test, Finsupp.single_apply, hn, sq_nonneg]
  have Family.test_le_l1 (F : T.Family) (u : ι →₀ ℝ) :
      ‖F.test u‖ ≤ u.sum (fun _ r => |r|) := by
    classical
    calc
      ‖F.test u‖ = ‖∑ v ∈ u.support, F.test (Finsupp.single v (u v))‖ := by
        rw [← map_sum]
        congr 2
        exact (Finsupp.sum_single u).symm
      _ ≤ ∑ v ∈ u.support, ‖F.test (Finsupp.single v (u v))‖ := norm_sum_le _ _
      _ ≤ ∑ v ∈ u.support, |u v| := Finset.sum_le_sum fun v _ => Family.test_single_le F v (u v)
      _ = u.sum (fun _ r => |r|) := rfl
  have families_bddAbove : BddAbove (Set.range (Family.seminorm (T := T))) := by
    rw [Seminorm.bddAbove_range_iff]
    intro u
    exact ⟨u.sum (fun _ r => |r|), by rintro _ ⟨F, rfl⟩; exact Family.test_le_l1 F u⟩
  have test_le_primal (F : T.Family) (u : ι →₀ ℝ) :
      ‖F.test u‖ ≤ T.primalSeminorm u := by
    rw [primalSeminorm, Seminorm.iSup_apply families_bddAbove]
    exact le_ciSup (⟨u.sum (fun _ r => |r|), by
      rintro _ ⟨G, rfl⟩; exact Family.test_le_l1 G u⟩ :
      BddAbove (Set.range (fun G : T.Family => G.seminorm u))) F
  have coordinate_le_primal (u : ι →₀ ℝ) (v : ι) : |u v| ≤ T.primalSeminorm u := by
    have h := test_le_primal (T.singletonFamily v) u
    rw [EuclideanSpace.norm_eq] at h
    change Real.sqrt (∑ _ : Fin 1, ‖∑ w ∈ ({v} : Finset ι), u w‖ ^ 2) ≤ _ at h
    simpa [Real.sqrt_sq_eq_abs] using h
  have primal_eq_zero_iff (u : ι →₀ ℝ) : T.primalSeminorm u = 0 ↔ u = 0 := by
    constructor
    · intro h
      ext v
      have := coordinate_le_primal u v
      simpa [h] using this
    · rintro rfl
      exact map_zero _
  exact AddGroupNorm.toNormedAddCommGroup
    { T.primalSeminorm.toAddGroupSeminorm with
      eq_zero_of_map_eq_zero' := fun u h => (primal_eq_zero_iff u).mp h }

instance : NormedSpace ℝ T.Primal where
  norm_smul_le c u := le_of_eq (T.primalSeminorm.smul' c u)

abbrev JamesSpace := UniformSpace.Completion T.Primal

/-- Its full real continuous dual. -/
abbrev Dual := StrongDual ℝ T.JamesSpace

/-- Coordinate evaluation on finite arrays, bounded by the singleton test. -/
def coordinateFinite (v : ι) : T.Primal →L[ℝ] ℝ := by
  have Family.test_apply (F : T.Family) (u : ι →₀ ℝ) (i : Fin F.n) :
      F.test u i = ∑ v ∈ F.seg i, u v := rfl
  have Family.seminorm_apply (F : T.Family) (u : ι →₀ ℝ) :
      F.seminorm u = ‖F.test u‖ := rfl
  have Family.test_single_le (F : T.Family) (v : ι) (r : ℝ) :
      ‖F.test (Finsupp.single v r)‖ ≤ |r| := by
    classical
    rw [EuclideanSpace.norm_eq]
    apply (Real.sqrt_le_left (abs_nonneg r)).2
    by_cases h : ∃ i, v ∈ F.seg i
    · obtain ⟨i, hi⟩ := h
      have hne : ∀ j, j ≠ i → v ∉ F.seg j := by
        intro j hji hj
        exact Finset.disjoint_left.mp (F.disjoint hji) hj hi
      rw [Finset.sum_eq_single i]
      · simp [Family.test, Finsupp.single_apply, hi]
      · intro j _ hji
        simp [Family.test, Finsupp.single_apply, hne j hji]
      · simp
    · have hn : ∀ i, v ∉ F.seg i := by simpa using h
      simp [Family.test, Finsupp.single_apply, hn, sq_nonneg]
  have Family.test_le_l1 (F : T.Family) (u : ι →₀ ℝ) :
      ‖F.test u‖ ≤ u.sum (fun _ r => |r|) := by
    classical
    calc
      ‖F.test u‖ = ‖∑ v ∈ u.support, F.test (Finsupp.single v (u v))‖ := by
        rw [← map_sum]
        congr 2
        exact (Finsupp.sum_single u).symm
      _ ≤ ∑ v ∈ u.support, ‖F.test (Finsupp.single v (u v))‖ := norm_sum_le _ _
      _ ≤ ∑ v ∈ u.support, |u v| := Finset.sum_le_sum fun v _ => Family.test_single_le F v (u v)
      _ = u.sum (fun _ r => |r|) := rfl
  have families_bddAbove : BddAbove (Set.range (Family.seminorm (T := T))) := by
    rw [Seminorm.bddAbove_range_iff]
    intro u
    exact ⟨u.sum (fun _ r => |r|), by rintro _ ⟨F, rfl⟩; exact Family.test_le_l1 F u⟩
  have test_le_primal (F : T.Family) (u : ι →₀ ℝ) :
      ‖F.test u‖ ≤ T.primalSeminorm u := by
    rw [primalSeminorm, Seminorm.iSup_apply families_bddAbove]
    exact le_ciSup (⟨u.sum (fun _ r => |r|), by
      rintro _ ⟨G, rfl⟩; exact Family.test_le_l1 G u⟩ :
      BddAbove (Set.range (fun G : T.Family => G.seminorm u))) F
  have coordinate_le_primal (u : ι →₀ ℝ) (v : ι) : |u v| ≤ T.primalSeminorm u := by
    have h := test_le_primal (T.singletonFamily v) u
    rw [EuclideanSpace.norm_eq] at h
    change Real.sqrt (∑ _ : Fin 1, ‖∑ w ∈ ({v} : Finset ι), u w‖ ^ 2) ≤ _ at h
    simpa [Real.sqrt_sq_eq_abs] using h
  exact (show T.Primal →ₗ[ℝ] ℝ from Finsupp.lapply v).mkContinuous 1 (by
      intro u
      change ‖(show ι →₀ ℝ from u) v‖ ≤ 1 * T.primalSeminorm u
      simpa only [one_mul, Real.norm_eq_abs] using coordinate_le_primal u v)

def coordinate (v : ι) : T.Dual :=
  (T.coordinateFinite v).extend UniformSpace.Completion.toComplL

/-- Primal unit coordinate and its canonical image in the completion. -/
def unitFinite (v : ι) : T.Primal := Finsupp.single v 1

def unitVector (v : ι) : T.JamesSpace := (T.unitFinite v : T.JamesSpace)

def headDual (H : Finset ι) : T.Dual →L[ℝ] T.Dual :=
  ∑ v ∈ H, (ContinuousLinearMap.apply ℝ ℝ (T.unitVector v)).smulRight (T.coordinate v)

/-- The norm-closed coordinate span, not the full infinite-tree dual. -/
def CoordinatePredual : Submodule ℝ T.Dual :=
  (Submodule.span ℝ (Set.range T.coordinate)).topologicalClosure

end SegmentSystem

/-- A finite interval in an ancestral order.  This keeps the root in every tree. -/
def intervalSystem {ι : Type*} (r : ι → ι → Prop)
    (hrefl : ∀ a, r a a) (hanti : ∀ a b, r a b → r b a → a = b) : SegmentSystem ι where
  IsSegment s := ∃ a b, r a b ∧ ∀ v, v ∈ s ↔ r a v ∧ r v b
  singleton v := by
    refine ⟨v, v, hrefl v, ?_⟩
    intro w
    simp only [Finset.mem_singleton]
    constructor
    · rintro rfl
      exact ⟨hrefl _, hrefl _⟩
    · intro h
      exact hanti w v h.2 h.1

/-- An ancestral partial order: the predecessors of any vertex form a chain. -/
class Ancestry (ι : Type*) extends PartialOrder ι where
  predecessors_total : ∀ {a b c : ι}, le a c → le b c → le a b ∨ le b a

namespace Ancestry

open scoped Classical
variable {ι : Type*} [A : Ancestry ι]
local instance : PartialOrder ι := A.toPartialOrder

/-- Nonempty finite intervals for an ancestral order. -/
def segments : SegmentSystem ι :=
  intervalSystem (· ≤ ·) (fun _ => le_rfl) (fun _ _ => le_antisymm)

end Ancestry

def ForestNode := {hw : ℕ × List ℕ // 1 ≤ hw.1 ∧ hw.2.length ≤ hw.1}

def ForestAncestor (a b : ForestNode) : Prop :=
  a.val.1 = b.val.1 ∧ a.val.2.IsPrefix b.val.2

@[instance_reducible] def forestAncestry : Ancestry ForestNode where
  le := ForestAncestor
  lt a b := ForestAncestor a b ∧ ¬ ForestAncestor b a
  le_refl a := ⟨rfl, List.prefix_refl _⟩
  le_trans _ _ _ hab hbc := ⟨hab.1.trans hbc.1, hab.2.trans hbc.2⟩
  le_antisymm a b hab hba := by
    apply Subtype.ext
    exact Prod.ext hab.1 (hab.2.sublist.antisymm hba.2.sublist)
  predecessors_total hab hac := by
    rcases List.prefix_or_prefix_of_prefix hab.2 hac.2 with h | h
    · exact Or.inl ⟨hab.1.trans hac.1.symm, h⟩
    · exact Or.inr ⟨hac.1.trans hab.1.symm, h⟩

def forestSegments : SegmentSystem ForestNode :=
  Ancestry.segments (A := forestAncestry)

/-- Prefix order on all finite words, explicitly distinct from their lexicographic order. -/
@[instance_reducible] def infiniteAncestry : Ancestry (List ℕ) where
  le := List.IsPrefix
  lt a b := a.IsPrefix b ∧ ¬ b.IsPrefix a
  le_refl := List.prefix_refl
  le_trans _ _ _ := List.IsPrefix.trans
  le_antisymm _ _ hab hba := hab.sublist.antisymm hba.sublist
  predecessors_total := List.prefix_or_prefix_of_prefix

/-- The infinite-height tree of all finite words; this includes the empty root. -/
def infiniteSegments : SegmentSystem (List ℕ) :=
  Ancestry.segments (A := infiniteAncestry)

abbrev ForestDual := forestSegments.Dual
abbrev InfinitePredual := infiniteSegments.CoordinatePredual

namespace Diamond

/-- Stage edges, retaining their parent, child number, and side. -/
@[reducible] def Edge : ℕ → Type
  | 0 => Unit
  | n + 1 => Edge n × ℕ × Bool

/-- At every replacement every parent edge receives countably many distinct new vertices. -/
@[reducible] def Vertex : ℕ → Type
  | 0 => Bool
  | n + 1 => Vertex n ⊕ (Edge n × ℕ)

def endpoint : (n : ℕ) → Edge n → Bool → Vertex n
  | 0, _, b => b
  | n + 1, (e, _, false), false => Sum.inl (endpoint n e false)
  | _ + 1, (e, i, false), true => Sum.inr (e, i)
  | _ + 1, (e, i, true), false => Sum.inr (e, i)
  | n + 1, (e, _, true), true => Sum.inl (endpoint n e true)

/-- The undirected replacement graph. -/
def graph (n : ℕ) : SimpleGraph (Vertex n) :=
  SimpleGraph.fromRel (fun u v => ∃ e : Edge n, u = endpoint n e false ∧ v = endpoint n e true)

/-- Final edges have length 2⁻ᵏ: this is the shortest-path metric of the normalized graph. -/
def distance (k : ℕ) (u v : Vertex k) : ℝ :=
  ((graph k).dist u v : ℝ) / (Nat.cast 2 : ℝ) ^ k

end Diamond

theorem headline_all_pairs :
    (∀ (k : ℕ) (f : Diamond.Vertex k → ForestDual) (D s : ℝ),
      0 < s → (∀ u v,s*Diamond.distance k u v ≤ ‖f u-f v‖ ∧
        ‖f u-f v‖ ≤ D*s*Diamond.distance k u v) → 1+(k:ℝ)/4 ≤ D^2) ∧
    (∀ (k : ℕ) (f : Diamond.Vertex k → InfinitePredual) (D s : ℝ),
      0 < s → (∀ u v,s*Diamond.distance k u v ≤ ‖f u-f v‖ ∧
        ‖f u-f v‖ ≤ D*s*Diamond.distance k u v) → 1+(k:ℝ)/4 ≤ D^2) := by

  sorry

end DiamondDistortion

end

end OAI
```

```json
      "statement_lean": "instance : NormedAddCommGroup T.Primal := by\n  have Family.test_apply (F : T.Family) (u : ι →₀ ℝ) (i : Fin F.n) :\n      F.test u i = ∑ v ∈ F.seg i, u v := rfl\n  have Family.seminorm_apply (F : T.Family) (u : ι →₀ ℝ) :\n      F.seminorm u = ‖F.test u‖ := rfl\n  have Family.test_single_le (F : T.Family) (v : ι) (r : ℝ) :\n      ‖F.test (Finsupp.single v r)‖ ≤ |r| := by\n    classical\n    rw [EuclideanSpace.norm_eq]\n    apply (Real.sqrt_le_left (abs_nonneg r)).2\n    by_cases h : ∃ i, v ∈ F.seg i\n    · obtain ⟨i, hi⟩ := h\n      have hne : ∀ j, j ≠ i → v ∉ F.seg j := by\n        intro j hji hj\n        exact Finset.disjoint_left.mp (F.disjoint hji) hj hi\n      rw [Finset.sum_eq_single i]\n      · simp [Family.test, Finsupp.single_apply, hi]\n      · intro j _ hji\n        simp [Family.test, Finsupp.single_apply, hne j hji]\n      · simp\n    · have hn : ∀ i, v ∉ F.seg i := by simpa using h\n      simp [Family.test, Finsupp.single_apply, hn, sq_nonneg]\n  have Family.test_le_l1 (F : T.Family) (u : ι →₀ ℝ) :\n      ‖F.test u‖ ≤ u.sum (fun _ r => |r|) := by\n    classical\n    calc\n      ‖F.test u‖ = ‖∑ v ∈ u.support, F.test (Finsupp.single v (u v))‖ := by\n        rw [← map_sum]\n        congr 2\n        exact (Finsupp.sum_single u).symm\n      _ ≤ ∑ v ∈ u.support, ‖F.test (Finsupp.single v (u v))‖ := norm_sum_le _ _\n      _ ≤ ∑ v ∈ u.support, |u v| := Finset.sum_le_sum fun v _ => Family.test_single_le F v (u v)\n      _ = u.sum (fun _ r => |r|) := rfl\n  have families_bddAbove : BddAbove (Set.range (Family.seminorm (T := T))) := by\n    rw [Seminorm.bddAbove_range_iff]\n    intro u\n    exact ⟨u.sum (fun _ r => |r|), by rintro _ ⟨F, rfl⟩; exact Family.test_le_l1 F u⟩\n  have test_le_primal (F : T.Family) (u : ι →₀ ℝ) :\n      ‖F.test u‖ ≤ T.primalSeminorm u := by\n    rw [primalSeminorm, Seminorm.iSup_apply families_bddAbove]\n    exact le_ciSup (⟨u.sum (fun _ r => |r|), by\n      rintro _ ⟨G, rfl⟩; exact Family.test_le_l1 G u⟩ :\n      BddAbove (Set.range (fun G : T.Family => G.seminorm u))) F\n  have coordinate_le_primal (u : ι →₀ ℝ) (v : ι) : |u v| ≤ T.primalSeminorm u := by\n    have h := test_le_primal (T.singletonFamily v) u\n    rw [EuclideanSpace.norm_eq] at h\n    change Real.sqrt (∑ _ : Fin 1, ‖∑ w ∈ ({v} : Finset ι), u w‖ ^ 2) ≤ _ at h\n    simpa [Real.sqrt_sq_eq_abs] using h\n  have primal_eq_zero_iff (u : ι →₀ ℝ) : T.primalSeminorm u = 0 ↔ u = 0 := by\n    constructor\n    · intro h\n      ext v\n      have := coordinate_le_primal u v\n      simpa [h] using this\n    · rintro rfl\n      exact map_zero _\n  exact AddGroupNorm.toNormedAddCommGroup\n    { T.primalSeminorm.toAddGroupSeminorm with\n      eq_zero_of_map_eq_zero' := fun u h => (primal_eq_zero_iff u).mp h }"

exact Family.test_le_l1 F u⟩\n  have test_le_primal (F : T.Family) (u : ι →₀ ℝ) :\n      ‖F.test u‖ ≤ T.primalSeminorm u := by\n    rw [primalSeminorm, Seminorm.iSup_apply families_bddAbove]\n    exact le_ciSup (⟨u.sum (fun _ r => |r|), by\n      rintro _ ⟨G, rfl⟩;       "name": "intervalSystem",
      "statement_lean": "def intervalSystem {ι : Type*} (r : ι → ι → Prop)\n    (hrefl : ∀ a, r a a) (hanti : ∀ a b, r a b → r b a → a = b) : SegmentSystem ι where\n  IsSegment s := ∃ a b, r a b ∧ ∀ v, v ∈ s ↔ r a v ∧ r v b\n  singleton v := by\n    refine ⟨v, v, hrefl v, ?_⟩\n    intro w\n    simp only [Finset.mem_singleton]\n    constructor\n    · rintro rfl\n      exact ⟨hrefl _, hrefl _⟩\n    · intro h\n      exact hanti w v h.2 h.1"

    },

    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "forestAncestry",
      "statement_lean": "@[instance_reducible] def forestAncestry : Ancestry ForestNode where\n  le := ForestAncestor\n  lt a b := ForestAncestor a b ∧ ¬ ForestAncestor b a\n  le_refl a := ⟨rfl, List.prefix_refl _⟩\n  le_trans _ _ _ hab hbc := ⟨hab.1.trans hbc.1, hab.2.trans hbc.2⟩\n  le_antisymm a b hab hba := by\n    apply Subtype.ext\n    exact Prod.ext hab.1 (hab.2.sublist.antisymm hba.2.sublist)\n  predecessors_total hab hac := by\n    rcases List.prefix_or_prefix_of_prefix hab.2 hac.2 with h | h\n    · exact Or.inl ⟨hab.1.trans hac.1.symm, h⟩\n    · exact Or.inr ⟨hac.1.trans hab.1.symm, h⟩"

      "name": "endpoint",
      "statement_lean": "def endpoint : (n : ℕ) → Edge n → Bool → Vertex n\n  | 0, _, b => b\n  | n + 1, (e, _, false), false => Sum.inl (endpoint n e false)\n  | _ + 1, (e, i, false), true => Sum.inr (e, i)\n  | _ + 1, (e, i, true), false => Sum.inr (e, i)\n  | n + 1, (e, _, true), true => Sum.inl (endpoint n e true)"
```
